I just need to get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this restlessness and need to move even when you're trying to fall asleep or during the night, or does it mostly happen while you're awake during the day?\n\nPatient: Mostly when I’m awake, I think. If I’m at church, or here waiting, or even at home, I start feeling trapped and I gotta get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot or feeling anxious about things, even when there isn’t a clear reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I don’t think I’m worried for no reason all the time. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: Does this feeling of restlessness or needing to move happen all the time, or only in certain situations like when you're in a quiet place or trying to relax?\n\nPatient: It’s… most of the time, yeah. Quiet places make it worse, like church or sitting here waiting. But even at home, if I’m supposed to just sit, I start feeling trapped and need to get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried about a number of different things, like work, health, or family, even when there’s no immediate reason to be?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I got things on my mind sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting around worrying about all that for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel especially anxious or uncomfortable in social situations, like when you're around people you don't know well or when you feel like you're being watched or judged?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I don’t love being watched or anything, but I wouldn’t say I get especially anxious around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to move your legs or body even when you’re not trying to, like there’s a need to keep moving even if you don’t want to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s pretty much why I came in. It feels like there’s a motor going inside me most of the day. If I have to sit still—church, waiting here, even at home—I start feeling trapped, like I need to get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood, like feeling down, irritable, or losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really. I get irritated sometimes, sure, but I haven’t really noticed being down or losing interest like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice physical symptoms like a fast heartbeat, sweating, or shaking when you feel this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really paid attention to that. Maybe I get tense, but I can’t say I’ve noticed my heart racing or shaking or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sudden waves of fear or discomfort that come on strong and make you feel like something bad is going to happen, even if it doesn’t make sense at the time?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I get uncomfortable, but not like sudden fear that something bad’s gonna happen.\n\nDoctor: When you feel that need to keep moving, do you also feel mentally tense or uneasy inside, like you can't relax even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. It’s like I got a motor going inside me, and if I’m sitting too long—like at church or here—I start feeling trapped. I just need to get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this restlessness and need to move even when you're trying to fall asleep or during the night, or does it mostly happen while you're awake during the day?\n\nPatient: Mostly when I’m awake, I think. During the day it’s like… there’s a motor running in me. If I gotta sit still at church, or here, or even at home, I start feeling trapped and need to get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced this feeling of restlessness or needing to move as a side effect from any medications you may have taken?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed it being from medicine. I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: When you try to rest or relax, do you feel inner tension or anxiety that makes it hard for you to stay still or calm?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. When I try to just sit and rest, it’s like there’s this motor going inside me. I start feeling kind of trapped, like I gotta get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Is the feeling of restlessness and needing to move always present, even when you're not trying to relax or sit still, or does it mostly happen when you're at rest or trying to be still?\n\nPatient: It’s kind of there most of the day, yeah… like a motor running. But I notice it worse when I’m supposed to sit still, like church or here at the clinic. Then I feel trapped and gotta get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: When you feel that need to move, is it mostly because you feel physically uncomfortable or restless, without necessarily feeling anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly it’s just my body feeling restless. Like there’s a motor in me. If I gotta sit still—at church, here, even at home—I start feeling trapped and I need to get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: When you feel that need to move, are you usually moving with a specific purpose, or does it feel more like an urge to move without really knowing why or where you need to go?\n\nPatient: It’s not really like I’m going somewhere for a reason. It’s more just… I gotta move. Like something’s running inside me, and if I sit too long I feel trapped, so I get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing, even if just for a short time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really, I guess. I’m restless a lot, but I don’t think I’ve had times like that, with not needing sleep or anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s more just… I gotta move. Like something’s running inside me, and if I sit too long I feel trapped, so I get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing, even if just for a short time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really, I guess.
